(e) weight superposition — with U^X, U^Y the superposition maps of two adjacent layers, ideal weights map as W*_{ij} e_i⊗e_j → W*_{ij} U^X_i⊗U^Y_j ≈ W, i.e. U^W = U^X⊗U^Y, so interference between two weights is the Frobenius inner product of their Kronecker products, and — the notable asymmetry — weights can interfere constructively where feature interference only ever seems to hurt; (f) attention-head superposition — attentional features are linear combinations of heads attending between a token pair and attentional circuits are the computation a head implements (skip-trigrams for a one-layer model), and training small one-layer transformers with trivial embeddings on data that over-represent OV-incoherent skip-trigrams at higher count than heads shows each trigram stored across at least two heads, so a head read alone yields a misleading partial mechanism and wiring diagrams quickly become unreadable; (g) feature-manifold toy model — features at evenly spaced angles φ around a circle, each data point drawn at angle θ and magnitude m activates φ as m·cos((φ−θ)/ℓ) within a quarter period in units of the resolution scale ℓ, and sweeping ℓ yields emergent discretisation (many localised overlapping units) that they connect to curve-detector families and to value-versus-variable coding in neuroscience, with the caveat that the finest discretisation may be an optimisation failure; the framing suggestion worth keeping is that "emergent discretisation" of a continuum may be what they have meant by "feature" all along (there are no numbers, baselines or ablations in the post and the authors ask to be read as lab-meeting notes)
- anthropic-com-research-language-models-mostly-know-what-they-know | Language Models (Mostly) Know What They Know (Kadavath, Conerly, Askell … Kaplan, Anthropic, 2022-07-11, arXiv:2207.05221): honesty as an umbrella (truthfulness, calibration, self-knowledge, explainability, non-deceptiveness) with self-knowledge treated as the generalisation test bed; four model sizes 800M/3B/12B/52B at 850B tokens plus two 12B twins deliberately pretrained on *different data mixtures* as a model-specificity control; three layers of experiment — (a) format engineering, rewriting all of BIG Bench, MMLU, TruthfulQA, LogiQA and QuALITY so options are visible in the prompt and the model only emits a letter label, then reading the letter probabilities, versus the default BIG Bench format where the model retypes the answer; (b) training-free self-evaluation where a T=1 sample is re-presented in a True/False frame and the "(A)" probability is read as P(True), with a variant that first shows five of the model's own brainstormed samples; (c) a value head fine-tuned into the model to predict P(IK), with ground truth built by sampling 30 answers at T=1 per question and duplicating each question once per correct sample as a many-hard-labels approximation of a soft label (batch 7680, ⅓ pretraining LR, loss at the final token only, body fine-tuned too); judged by equal-count-binned calibration charts over *all* option probabilities (not just the argmax), ECE, RMS calibration error, AUROC against binarised ground-truth P(IK), Brier score, and the operationally meaningful conditional accuracy on the subset the model itself marked P(True)>0.5, with appendices running sample-loss, answer-entropy over 200 samples and stuffed-context loss as training-free baselines; findings — very good calibration on lettered multiple choice across all five suites including TruthfulQA and LogiQA, improving with size and with few-shot, with the option-as-single-token format doing most of the work and no correlation between accuracy and calibration across BIG Bench subtasks, while BOTH perturbations that force an independent judgement break it ("none of the above" sharply cuts accuracy and calibration because even 52B under-uses that option; proposed-answer True/False is well calibrated but overconfident in the tails); RLHF policies look badly miscalibrated and are largely repaired by one temperature of 2.5 across three evaluations; zero-shot P(True) sits near 50% and needs few-shot to mean anything, brainstormed sibling samples help clearly on short-answer tasks but little on Codex/GSM8k, the base-vs-conditional accuracy gap grows with scale (verification improving faster than generation), and samples from smaller models are easier to categorise than one's own because self-samples sit near the decision boundary; P(IK) trained on TriviaQA alone gives 52B AUROC/Brier of 0.864/0.151 trivia, 0.928/0.194 mixed arithmetic, 0.687/0.164 Python function synthesis, 0.606/0.431 Lambada and 0.624/0.200 held-out GSM8k, versus 0.873/0.145, 0.987/0.042, 0.881/0.109, 0.853/0.108 and 0.752/0.121 when trained across tasks — so discrimination transfers and calibration does not, with OOD being systematically underconfident (and Lambada catastrophic because the head emits uniformly low scores); the prompt-intervention ladder shows P(IK) tracking availability rather than difficulty (obscure rodeo-hall question 18% → 78% once the Wikipedia article is prepended, larger gains for shorter articles, P(IK) rising monotonically with the revealed fraction of a correct GSM8k hint, lower for incorrect hints and dropping for distractor hints pulled from other questions, with the model only partially fooled by bad hints); self-specificity is real but small — twin 12B models score their own correct questions higher (0.463 vs 0.408 for A; 0.409 vs 0.477 for B, ~6% apart) and matching-checkpoint initialisation helps only on that model's own ground truth; the entropy baseline *degrades* with scale on HumanEval because bigger models give diverse yet correct programs, so diversity is not confidence; limits are named honestly — pretrained models only, five sampling datasets, no way to separate truth from what humans say, and no account of deliberate deception, which would defeat the whole trend
- transformer-circuits-pub-2022-solu-index-html | Softmax Linear Units (Elhage et al., Anthropic, 2022-06-27): replaces the MLP activation with SoLU(x) = x·softmax(x) plus a post-activation LayerNorm, motivated entirely by making MLP neurons legible rather than by performance — the framing being that a lab is both the reverse engineer and the hardware designer, so architectures can be chosen for how well they can be taken apart; softmax makes large components suppress small ones, so a basis-aligned feature survives (4,0,0,0)→(4,0,0,0) while one spread across dimensions shrinks ((1,1,1,1)→¼ each), which supplies lateral inhibition, approximate sparsity and superlinearity in one line; judged by blinded human rating (neurons drawn at random from baseline and SoLU models across 1/16/24/40/64 layers, ~20 training-distribution snippets with firing tokens shaded by magnitude, 1-2 minutes to decide whether a single rule covers ≥80% of the strongest firings, 660 neurons total, plus a 16L model with the LayerNorm but no SoLU to split the two changes) and by a loss-to-effective-model-size conversion read off the baseline scaling curve; result is 35% → 60% explainable neurons for 1-40L (a 1.7× relative gain) with performance inside 0.95-1.05× effective size at every scale (1.01× at 50B), under 1% training slowdown, and downstream benchmarks mixed but not systematically worse (arithmetic favoured SoLU, TriviaQA the baseline), while SoLU without the LayerNorm costs 30-50% of effective size, worse at larger scales; the gain collapses at 64L/50B (42% vs 33%) either because bigger models hold concepts too sophisticated for two minutes of inspection or because deep-model optimisation dynamics change; the LayerNorm turns out to be the catch, since it is scale-invariant and therefore rescales exactly the distributed features SoLU suppressed — an unblinded hand-categorisation of ~1,000 examples for one "left/right body part" neuron shows post-LayerNorm examples picking up unrelated low-activation cases, i.e. small-activation superposition smuggled back through, which the authors read as the first real though circumstantial evidence for the superposition hypothesis because restoring the ability to superpose is what restored the loss; qualitative layer map is de-tokenize (multi-token words, compound words, proper nouns, LaTeX heads, and context/language-disambiguated tokens such as three separate neurons for "die" in German, Dutch and Afrikaans) → abstract middle features (numbers only when enumerating people; clauses describing sound, clothing, music; emphasis and hedging discourse markers; A/B/C/D as grades) → re-tokenize (a late neuron firing on "st" and lifting "rag" so a word can be emitted as an out-of-vocabulary sequence), with neuron splitting across scale, neuron families parameterised by (token X, language Y), and early/late duality as recurring structures; the authors are explicit that this measures "interpretable at first glance" with no negative examples, that one base architecture and dataset were used, that even fully interpretable neurons would not make a frontier model globally understandable, and post-publication that strong-activation monosemanticity does not license a whole-neuron claim (Transformer Circuits thread, Anthropic)
- anthropic-com-research-scaling-laws-and-interpretability-of-learning-from-repeated-data | Scaling Laws and Interpretability of Learning from Repeated Data (Hernandez et al., Anthropic, 2022-05, arXiv:2205.10487): mostly-unique training stream plus a repeated slice of the original data at a fixed 100B-token budget, sweeping model size, slice size and repeat frequency over 2-3 orders of magnitude each; every loss is re-parameterised into an effective-model-size multiplier by fitting a power law on control runs, which is what makes the heterogeneous sweep comparable — an 800M model with 10% repeated tokens at ~100 repeats of a 0.1% slice lands near 340M-equivalent, and at 90% repeated data the learning curve is a literal double descent (rising then falling again) that flattens into a long plateau at 50%; damage is uneven across capabilities in the direction of generalisation, 3% repeated data costing up to 3x effective size on copying a repeated Harry Potter paragraph but at most 1.15x on test loss, with prefix matching at ~2x, and at the peak loss on the 11th consecutive copy stops improving at all, i.e. copying stopped working rather than merely weakened; one-layer attention-only models lose the pure skip-trigram target of repeated proper names (control >97% on the continuation where the repeated model gives <4%) and two-layer models lose fuzzy copying, which the authors read as a skip-trigram head displacing the induction head; both boundaries of the degraded region fit E = k·N^α (right 5.1e7/−0.50, left 4.2e6/−0.56), extrapolating to damage from as few as 2 repeats at hundreds of billions of parameters with a fixed token count, and the out-of-distribution Python loss only drops materially at 50-90% repetition (0.84/0.75 multipliers); pre-training on repeated data ossifies the model — fine-tuning leaves it 1.6x worse in effective size than training from scratch and it eventually forgets the repeated subset, so the authors call it strictly dominated; the incentive arithmetic in discussion (0.9·2.2 + 0.1·0 = 1.98 < 2.0) is the stated reason zero loss on even a 10% slice pays for a 2x capacity penalty; limits are named openly — fixed token count pre-Chinchilla, fits so noisy that the 1% scan was excluded and the bad-region boundaries are called useful rather than accurate, a random (not high-quality-curated) repeated slice, loss instead of downstream evaluations, no regularisation sweep — and logit attribution on the two-layer models was inconclusive about whether the induction head fired less or was out-competed, with the authors finding per-token losses more informative than circuit-level attribution
- github-com-anthropics-pysvelte | PySvelte (Anthropic, 2021): a deliberately unsupportable sample library that bridges the Python research side and the browser visualisation side — a Svelte component placed in `src/` becomes callable from Python under the same name with keyword-argument completion taken from an optional companion Python file that carries the docstring, argument types and Python-side assertions (so malformed data fails before rendering), builds are triggered from Python so no npm step is needed, NumPy arrays arrive on the JavaScript side as ndarrays, components render automatically when last in a notebook cell or via an explicit show call, rendered HTML objects concatenate into pages, and a publish call writes shareable pages to a bucket with optional chat announcement; the worked example is an internal attention-pattern component for self-attention blocks, a variant of which renders in the transformer-circuits framework paper; the whole of the evidence is a design-goals list — bespoke visualisation, modular reuse, easy publication, usability by researchers with no web background — and the README leads with a warning that many features will not work without a lab-specific configuration file (README only; source and component docs not read)
- transformer-circuits-pub-2021-garcon-index-html | Garçon: the infrastructure paper (Anthropic, 2021-12) — for interpretability on models too large for a notebook — a server launched from a model snapshot path autodetects config and GPU count, clients connect from a notebook or script (a cell that finds a running server reconnects; idle servers self-shutdown after an hour), and the model exposes named *probe points* at which a user-supplied probe function receives the tensor and a save context and returns a replacement or nothing, with the functions shipped via a Python-object serializer so arbitrary code runs beside the tensor; forward passes return logits by default and backward passes take gradients on a user loss; three stated design goals are only-capture-on-request (saving every intermediate for a large model is enormous), reduce-on-server (smaller transfers, and per-GPU parallelism doing its own share), and stateful server-side accumulation (per-neuron top activations over potentially millions of corpus examples, where full round-trips would be orders of magnitude slower); one process per GPU runs in lockstep, a single rank listens and broadcasts, and a gather returns results; a simpler wrapper covers single-context analysis at less power, and parameters are addressed through the same naming scheme; the interface is presented as an assembly language for higher abstractions; judged by adoption rather than measurement — essentially all their interpretability work runs through it, "time to first plot" is named as the metric, ~1,000 lines but tightly coupled to their own model code and cluster, and the authors say the real contribution is organisational: every model the lab trains is addressable by the interpretability team by default (Anthropic; tool announcement accompanying A Mathematical Framework for Transformer Circuits)
- transformer-circuits-pub-2021-exercises-index-html | Transformer Circuit Exercises: a companion problem set to the transformer-circuits framework in which every head is written out as explicit weights — W_net = W_out·W_V collapses a head to one rank-limited content map applied after the routing matrix, so h_i(x) = Σ_j A_ij W_net x_j, and the cross-head product W_V(head 2)·W_out(head 1) with its singular values answers whether a later head reads an earlier head's write and how strongly per direction; Exercise 1 builds a two-head "virtual attention head" attending two tokens back on 7/8 previous-token plus 1/8 two-back capacity (two-token path = W_net²·W_net¹, one-token path = the same with identities, so the pair is a mixed sum), Exercise 2 hand-builds a pointer-arithmetic induction head from a content-component Q/K and a sin/cos position basis, where a 2-D rotation composed into the *query* turns a self-selecting head into a previous-token head and the reverse rotation into one landing on the token after the earlier copy (W_K unchanged, one scalar beta suppresses self-attention), and Exercise 3 rebuilds the same behaviour as previous-token K-composition because rotary-style encodings never reach W_V, so position must arrive as content copied into the second head's key; solutions stress non-uniqueness — an arbitrary rotation composed into both Q and K is functionally identical — and there are no trained models, metrics, baselines or ablations (Transformer Circuits thread, Anthropic; 2021-12)
- goodfire-com-research-interpreting-evo-2 | Interpreting Evo 2: BatchTopK SAEs on layer 26 (a StripedHyena layer) of Arc Institute's Evo 2 (7B / 40B, up to 1M base pairs at single-nucleotide resolution, trained across all domains of life), trained on a balanced eukaryotic/prokaryotic mix of high-quality reference genomes after ReLU SAEs showed no high-frequency feature pathology; layer chosen after an initial sweep over several layers including both block types, with the post's own explanation for why a late layer wins here being the tiny nucleotide vocabulary (less serial computation needed to commit a token); features were scored against canonical biological annotations with a domain-F1 measure and rendered as activation tracks over genomes beside existing annotation tracks, plus an E. coli region (thrT / tufB) where separate α-helix, β-sheet and tRNA features line up with the annotation and with an AlphaFold3 structure prediction of the same protein–RNA complex; named features span coding sequences, exon–intron boundaries, tRNA/rRNA, prophage and CRISPR elements; steering is described only as "early signs of life" toward new protein structures with an explicit note that steering is far harder here than in language models; no headline numbers, baselines or ablations in the post, and the preprint's interpretability sections (2.4, 4.4) were not read (Goodfire / Arc Institute)
- goodfire-com-research-under-the-hood-of-a-reasoning-model | Under the Hood of a Reasoning Model: the first public SAEs on a true reasoning model — DeepSeek-R1 at 671B, one trained on a custom reasoning corpus and one on OpenR1-Math, released alongside SQL dumps of each feature's max-activating examples so others can browse without running the model; five reasoning-specific features exhibited (backtracking = catching its own error and correcting course, reference-back to earlier statements, a period after subset/subsequence talk, an entity worth tracking, and a marker immediately before the result of a multi-step calculation); two steering observations the authors call new for reasoning models — naive steering from token zero fails and only works after R1 writes its "Okay, so the user has asked a question about…" frame, with attention sinks at the END of that frame rather than the start, which they read as R1 not counting its true response as begun until the preamble is done (that frame was so common in training traces — >95% of English reasoning traces open with "Okay," — that the model apparently treats it as prompt, and the authors report a strong feature-distribution shift between prompt-with-frame, thinking trace and answer); and oversteering is non-monotonic, so pushing some features far past the useful range returns the model to its unsteered behaviour before outputs finally become incoherent, hypothesised as the model detecting an over-perturbed internal state and course-correcting — with the warning that behaviour edits may be routed around; demonstrations are a division feature turning a "1024 times 2" prompt into a correct division trace answered 512, and an "answer quickly" feature pinning a long philosophical essay's reasoning down to a short plan-then-answer shape; no metrics, baselines, ablations or reconstruction statistics anywhere in the post, so it is an existence proof with curated examples only (Goodfire)
- goodfire-com-research-bsf-vision | Uncovering Neural Geometry in Vision Models With Block-Sparse Featurizers: a featurizer whose atom is a multi-dimensional subspace (a "block") rather than a direction, with sparsity enforced on blocks — a few blocks fire per input and all their directions fire together — trained purely on reconstruction; three variants (vanilla with block-wise TopK on each block's L2 norm, Grassmannian, group-lasso) all recover known manifolds from a toy activation set built by summing samples of manifolds in superposition, where an SAE fragments them even when handed a top-g-directions budget against a single width-g block, and imposing block-sparsity also improves two concurrent featurizers (MFA, SMixAE), with removing the ReLU mattering in every variant; on DINOv3 and SDXL it gives a more faithful account at lower minimum description length than an SAE and the blocks are dense connected clouds — an "arch" block spans bottom-of-arch to crown, another spans wrist to fingertips of a hand holding an object, a video's top blocks divide a wolf into body parts — which SAEs collapse to single directions, and grid-walking an SDXL "pretzel" subspace generates differently twisted and knotted pretzels while 2-D grids over other blocks vary hat brim angle / crown shape / orientation and a coffee's milk pattern, spoon position and cup design; in InceptionV1 a single block captures curve orientation as a continuum where neurons and SAE atoms give a fractured view, and the block's further components are higher-order Fourier harmonics of the angle, which explains the previously puzzling neurons that wrap every 180° instead of 360° as readers of the second harmonic; and the dimensionality question is answered for this model class — average stable rank across ~32,000 DINOv3 concepts rarely exceeds 4 in blocks of width 16, i.e. most concepts are two- to four-dimensional, consistent with 2-D projections of a 3-D world; limits are breadth (two vision-side models, ground truth only in the toy setting), the authors' own scoping that block-sparsity is unlikely to be the right prior in every domain (vision-language-action models are named), and the absence of any held-out behavioural prediction or intervention-recovery benchmark — evidence here is reconstruction, description and steering aesthetics (Goodfire / Harvard / Stanford / Brown; post only, arXiv paper not fetched)
- arxiv-2605.12412 | Stories in Space: In-Context Learning Trajectories in Conceptual Belief Space: Llama-3.1-8B reads SimpleStories prefixes; belief state = probability-weighted expected 0-10 rating of six emotions per sentence; layer-9 probes decode that curve at RMSE .09 (Emotions/Genres) and .11 (Arbitrary) from story text that never mentions the concept; behaviour-space and activation-space concept-distance matrices correlate r=.92/.89 (.71 for the unstructured Arbitrary control) and yield identical Ward hierarchies; emotion centroids match human valence-arousal ratings at r=.93; steering for one emotion drags its manifold neighbours (sadness→more anger, less happiness) and the size of that off-target effect is predicted by centroid distance (r=.65 Emotions, .42 Genres) and vanishes across top-level hierarchy clusters; probe-weight steering needs a 7-layer span because later layers undo a single-layer edit, whereas difference-in-means works at one layer with a smaller target effect (Goodfire / Stanford)
- goodfire-com-research-reward-hacking-activation-monitors | Models Know When They're Reward Hacking: difference-in-means "exploit fixture" direction from synthetic cheat/honest code examples; reward hacking in 50-96% of rollouts across Kimi K3 / GLM 5.2 / Qwen 3.8 Max × 3 agentic benchmarks; direction validated four ways (FineWeb max-activations about Goodhart/plagiarism-evasion, J-lens tokens "cheating/hack/sneak/hardcoded/unethical", exam-story steering produces justified cheating, honeypot take-up rises under steering); probe at matched FPR catches +3.1% more hacks than a CoT monitor on Kimi K3 but 7.9% fewer on GLM 5.2 on DeepSWE, while transferring from short synthetic coding examples to real long-context shopping/customer-support outputs and catching locally-innocuous hacks the LLM judge misses; resampling the trajectory from probe-firing deliberation points reproduces the hack at a far higher rate than from quiet points; probe+LLM cascade cuts monitoring cost 90% for ~1% precision (Goodfire)
- goodfire-com-research-covariance-pooling | Covariance-based Sequence Pooling: second-moment (XᵀX) sequence pooling with a learned linear bottleneck; +52.9% R² on 39-track genomic prediction and +8.4% GO AUC over mean pooling, NTv3; token identity/position provably cancel (Goodfire)
- alignment-anthropic-com-2025-distill-paraphrases | Scratchpad = text or channel? Paraphrase-distillation invariance (Anthropic Alignment Science)
- goodfire-com-research-reasoning-theater | Reasoning Theater: performative CoT = gap between CoT monitor and probe/forced-answer state (Goodfire / Harvard)
- anthropic-com-research-reasoning-models-dont-say-think | CoT faithfulness under planted hints: disclosure 25% / 39%; outcome RL plateaus at 28% / 20%; reward-hack admitted <2% (Anthropic Alignment Science)
- alignment-anthropic-com-2025-believe-it-or-not | Believe It or Not: belief depth = generality + robustness + representation (Anthropic Fellows)
- anthropic-com-research-introspection | Signs of Introspection in LLMs (concept injection; Anthropic)

- alignment-anthropic-com-2026-lie-detectors | Fine-Tuned Lie Detectors Failed to Generalize (MATS / Anthropic Fellows; OOD transfer failure)
- arxiv-2602.10067 | Features as Rewards: RLFR for open-ended tasks (Goodfire AI; probe reward, Gemma-3-12B)
- arxiv-2511.16035 | Liars' Bench: seven lie datasets on two axes (object of belief × reason for lying); no detector above chance everywhere (Cadenza Labs / Anthropic)
- anthropic-com-research-probes-catch-sleeper-agents | Simple probes can catch sleeper agents: one-contrast-pair residual direction, AUROC 99.3% vs 95.3% for the best of 1000 random directions; defection-eligibility is a mid-stack PCA axis (Anthropic Alignment Science)
- alignment-anthropic-com-2025-honesty-elicitation | Evaluating Honesty and Lie Detection Techniques: five dishonest-model testbeds, no task-specific supervision; honesty fine-tuning 27%→52% (+prompt 65%), self-classify 0.82→0.88 AUROC, truth-probe replication weak (Anthropic Alignment Science)
- alignment-anthropic-com-2025-cheap-monitors | Model-Internals Classifiers / Cost-Effective Constitutional Classifiers via Representation Re-use: probe vs partially fine-tuned classifiers compared on a log-space-AUROC/cost frontier; EMA probe ≈ 3% overhead, one retrained block ≈ 4%, mean pooling worse than the smallest dedicated model (Anthropic Alignment Science)
- arxiv-2601.04603 | Constitutional Classifiers++: exchange classifier + cascade + all-layer linear probe as first stage; 3.5% relative compute, 0.05% production refusal rate, 0.00505 high-risk vulns per 1k attempts, no universal jailbreak in 1,736 red-teaming hours (Anthropic)
- goodfire-com-research-verbalized-eval-awareness-inflates-measured-safety | Verbalized Eval Awareness Inflates Measured Safety: VEA in 8 models × 19 benchmarks (515 verified, 95% CoT-only); refusal +3-18pp with VEA; deleting VEA sentences +8% compliance (+34% for safety-keyword ones), injecting +12.6pp but ~+1pp when reframed as a helpfulness test; prompt rewrites cut VEA >60% (authority claims) / 75% (paraphrase) with refusal falling too; steering vectors partly insert a persona (Goodfire / UK AISI)
- goodfire-com-research-logits-as-a-new-monitor-for-evaluation-awareness | Logits as a new monitor for evaluation awareness: training-free monitor = sequence probability of one hand-picked eval-aware sentence, multiplied per token over one forward pass at every sentence boundary; Kimi K2.5 + Qwen 3 32B, Fortress/Petri vs WildChat; AUROC 0.79 from one rollout vs 0.75 for a GPT-5.4 VEA judge at 100, and 0.74/0.77 reading only the start of the CoT; verbalisation of that rollout explains 0.4% of monitor variance vs 24.9% for the prompt; dropping verbalising rollouts moves AUROC <0.02; "The user might be testing" correlates 0.59 with "This is clearly a test" but 0.08 with "This is a fictional scenario"; one sentence beats the judge whose flagged sentences almost never match it (13.3% / 0.2%)
- arxiv-2510.24256 | From Memorization to Reasoning in the Spectrum of Loss Curvature: K-FAC curvature basis of MLP weights; bottom of the spectrum carries recitation and brittle skills; label-free truncation to 60% curvature mass cuts strict recitation on unseen quotes to 16.1% vs 60.0% for the supervised BalancedSubnet baseline at lower perplexity; ViT memorised-label rate 81.6% → 3.5% while validation accuracy rises 67.0% → 71.7%; downstream retention falls monotonically logic 95-106% > open-book QA 93-99% > closed-book QA 74-86% > math 66-74% > memorisation 3-16%, and a task's activation overlap with the deleted low-curvature bands predicts its drop (Goodfire)
- arxiv-2511.01836 | Priors in Time: SAEs' sparsity terms are additive over timesteps, so their implied prior over latents is i.i.d. across time while LM activations are not (intrinsic dimension rises with position; up to ~80% of a token's variance is recoverable from the preceding window of up to 100); Temporal Feature Analysis splits each activation into an attention-over-past predictable code plus a sparse novel residual sharing one dictionary, matches SAE reconstruction (~0.139-0.152 NMSE) while its predictive code aligns with the slow half of the activation spectrum (CKA 0.75 vs 0.35-0.37 for SAEs) and the novel code with the fast half (0.75), recovers story event boundaries, the correct parse of garden-path sentences, the graph geometry of a random-walk in-context task, and user/assistant structure in OOD dialogue with trajectory tortuosity ~2.0 vs ~25-30, and supports code-space counterfactual edits on 4 of 9 graph nodes where no other code works at all (Goodfire / Harvard / EPFL)

- arxiv-2511.00617 | Belief Dynamics: ICL and activation steering are both belief updates = a·m + b + γ·N^(1−α) fit with 4 params per model/dataset; held-out r 0.98 (Llama-3.1-8B, 725 cells each) / 0.98 (Qwen-2.5-7B) / 0.97 (Gemma-2-9B) / 0.98 OOS (Llama-3.1-70B), high-prior concepts 0.87, 3-label flipped sentiment 0.96; crossover shot-count N*(m) predicted at r 0.97 while steering shifts ICL curves left/right by sign; one best steering layer per model (12/14/20) and a breakdown threshold past which large m returns the model to chance; multi-shot vectors steer weaker than zero-shot once norm-matched (Goodfire / Harvard / Stanford / NTT)
- goodfire-com-research-predicting-rare-llm-failures-with-30x-fewer-rollouts | Predicting Rare LLM Failures with 30× Fewer Rollouts / Logit Path Extrapolation: build interpolants M(α) between Qwen-3-4B-Abliterated (α=0) and Qwen-3-4B (α=1) by mixing per-token logits autoregressively, measure compliance rate where it is common, fit log₁₀(compliance) vs α on points below 50% and extrapolate to α=1; HarmBench 200 prompts × 100k precomputed rollouts, StrongREJECT rubric with GPT-5.4-mini judge, AUROC between "never complies in 100" and "complies at least once in 100k", averaged over 500 Monte Carlo draws; matches naive-sampling AUROC with 30× fewer rollouts (20× with 100 rollouts/α; 28× with a 10-rollout search step) and beats importance sampling; extrapolation is only valid where the target's failures lie on the path to the unsafe variant (Goodfire / MIT)
- arxiv-2608.19611 | Forking Fast: Forking Paths Analysis (resample continuations at every position of a greedy base path, aggregate final answers into an outcome distribution per timestep) is dominated by multinomial sampling noise rather than token-level sensitivity — replicate TVD between disjoint sample subsets falls with log–log slope −0.49 (predicted −1/2), measured within 2% of the exact i.i.d. null, and between-run ≈ within-run variation (0.0451 vs 0.0442) at S=200; a three-stage smoother (PELT change-point segmentation with an exact multinomial cost, Gaussian-kernel-weighted Dirichlet pooling truncated at segment boundaries, cross-validated penalty/bandwidth) multiplies effective sample size 22.1×/7.3×/1.6× at S=5/30/100 for one-token spacing, 5.0×/3.3×/1.0× at 4-token spacing and 4.9×/3.1×/1.1× for sentence-level resampling of the reasoning model, with excess TVD falling 0.0265 → 0.0056; stride plus smoothing cuts the budget to 1/8 at small error increase; gains shrink in forking regions (fork TVD 0.0669 → 0.0936 → 0.1587 as stride grows 4 → 8 → 16 at S=30, saturating ≈0.16 once the fork is skipped), the smoother loses to raw data at ε=0.20 forks, its 90% credible intervals under-cover (0.48-0.64), reference curves carry their own noise floor, and the authors flag a `ruptures` bug where a custom cost not inheriting `BaseCost` is silently swapped for least-squares (Goodfire; 1.77B tokens, Llama-3-8B-Instruct + DeepSeek-R1-Distill-Llama-8B, tinyMMLU n=100)
- goodfire-com-research-probe-based-data-attribution | Probe-Based Data Attribution: a natural (not poisoned) testbed plus an activation-similarity ranker for DPO data — OLMo 2 7B's SFT and DPO checkpoints diverge on [harmful request] + [distractor] prompts (compliance on the constrained version, refusal on the bare one; removed distractor makes both refuse; stronger in 32B), and datapoints are ranked by cosine between two mean-pooled SFT activation differences, behaviour = DPO-response minus SFT-response on the same prompt and datapoint = accepted minus rejected response, with the probe averaged over 150 Grok-4-generated prompts filtered so only DPO+distractor scores nonzero and validated as a layer-20 steering vector (coefficient 2.0) that raises compliance only when the distractor is present; retraining from the SFT checkpoint after filtering the top 30,000 of 378k pairs cuts the harmful response rate 63% with no GSM8k/IFEval/XSTest damage, swapping their accepted/rejected labels cuts it 78% at a small GSM8k cost, dropping all data from the four worst of 20 generator LLMs cuts it 84%, ranking costs ~12 H100-hours (~$30) versus 128 (~$320) for a LESS adaptation and $500 of GPT-5-mini judge calls, and the judge/LESS lead only at the smallest intervention sizes on the 120-prompt, 100-rollout, GPT-5-mini-scored held-out set; the probe's lowest-ranked pairs are the label-inversion case (accepted refuses, rejected complies), and an unsupervised Ward-clustered heatmap of test-prompt behaviour vectors against training-datapoint vectors surfaces the behaviour as aligned and inverted clusters alongside unrelated formatting and verbosity clusters (Goodfire / Caltech / SPAR; arXiv:2602.11079)

- **Discrimination transfers, calibration does not**: a P(IK) head trained only on trivia ranks held-out maths, code and fill-in-the-blank questions far above chance (AUROC 0.61-0.93) while its *numbers* are wrong (Brier up to 0.43, systematically underconfident). So "the readout carries the information" and "the readout's value is a probability" are two separate claims to test — and we should expect under- over over-confidence on new distributions, the reverse of the usual miscalibration story. (anthropic-com-research-language-models-mostly-know-what-they-know, arxiv-2511.16035)
- **Conditional accuracy at a self-set threshold** (accuracy on the subset the model itself labels P(True) > 0.5, versus base accuracy) is the cheapest honest statement of what a confidence readout buys, and it is a cleaner headline than AUROC because it is in the deployment's units. Its *growth with scale* — verification improving faster than generation — is itself a scaling-law-shaped prediction worth testing on our probes. (anthropic-com-research-language-models-mostly-know-what-they-know, goodfire-com-research-reasoning-theater)
- **Prompt-format is a first-order experimental variable in calibration claims**: the same questions move from poorly to well calibrated purely by presenting options as single letter tokens rather than answer strings, and two independence-forcing perturbations ("none of the above", proposed-answer True/False) both break calibration even at 52B. Any readout whose answer must be a discrete token should report the format as part of the result and never be compared across formats. (anthropic-com-research-language-models-mostly-know-what-they-know)
- **Availability ladder for prediction-under-intervention**: prepend the source document, then reveal a hint in fractions, then swap in a correct / incorrect / *distractor* hint. Only the distractor cell separates "tracks what the model can now use" from "tracks intrinsic question difficulty", and it costs one prompt edit — a reusable control for any readout we claim is about capability rather than item hardness. (anthropic-com-research-language-models-mostly-know-what-they-know, arxiv-2604.12493)
- **Self-knowledge claims need twin models on crossed ground truth**: two models with identical architecture but different pretraining data, each scoring questions that only it gets right, give a ~6% diagonal advantage — small, but the right instrument, and the same shape as the base-model-control and cross-model-matrix nulls. Sampling diversity (answer entropy) is not a substitute: it *inverts* with scale on code, where bigger models give many diverse correct answers. (anthropic-com-research-language-models-mostly-know-what-they-know, anthropic-com-research-scaling-laws-and-interpretability-of-learning-from-repeated-data)

## Patterns noticed

- **Do not assume the suppressor points at the concept being withheld; it points at what the mechanism is keyed by**: the natural hypothesis for a withheld answer — that refusal suppresses the features encoding the answer — came out measurably *near zero*, while the feature that was actually doing the damage interacted negatively with a completely different family (the key-side harm-detection features) whose contribution the selection heads had been relying on. This is the third time in the folder that a label's semantics drifted from its causal role (the bias-awareness feature raising gender bias, the "academic formatting" feature being a dataset artefact), and it generalises into a concrete test: when we claim "feature X suppresses behaviour Y", measure the interaction between X and *the features the downstream mechanism reads*, not between X and the concept Y is about — and calibrate against a random-pair null so that a null on the obvious hypothesis is publishable rather than left on the cutting-room floor. (transformer-circuits-pub-2025-november-update-index-html)
- **Asymmetry between the two directions of an intervention is evidence about distribution, not noise**: where one direction works with single-layer steering and the reverse direction only works when applied to a different target and spread over earlier layers, the mechanism is telling you the quantity is aggregated somewhere you have not looked — and the honest response is to report the sign dependence and the layer span as part of the result, then say plainly that the direction is a proxy. The same reading applies to the many steering results in this folder that report one direction and their own chosen scale, which is why an asymmetry check should be the default second arm rather than an extra. (transformer-circuits-pub-2025-november-update-index-html)
- **Perceptual and motor features are different objects, and the second is what steering needs**: this month's cross-modal post is the first place in the folder where the split is visible on outputs you can inspect by eye — a feature fires reliably on the right element of an ASCII or SVG face, and a *different* feature, whose top logits are the concept itself, is the one whose steering turns a smile into a frown through a neutral midpoint while perceptual features will not move the drawing at all. The authors state the asymmetry as an observation and immediately list "what characterises a steerable feature, and can we find them automatically" as future work, which means the field currently has no criterion: the practical rule is that a steering claim should start from a top-logit-effect ranking rather than from activation strength, and should say which of the two kinds of feature it is using. The second half of the pattern is the unfalsifiability to avoid: "we found a feature that fires on X" is a search with a guaranteed hit, so a discovery write-up owes a denominator — of N body-part features examined, how many peak on the correct part — and without it the evidence is a curated set of pictures with a compelling story attached. (transformer-circuits-pub-2025-october-update-index-html)
- **A feature is a detector for a shape-in-a-frame, so framing belongs to the input — and reordering is the cheapest causal test you can run**: minimal-context and context-displacement are a matched pair worth adding to every feature write-up, because the second one is what catches a feature that is really a position or proximity detector — a taller, richer input in which the target has simply moved out of scope switches the feature off, which no amount of relevance testing would reveal. The companion is element reordering on any ordered format (markup, code, tables, tool-call arguments, reasoning steps): permuting content while holding it fixed costs one forward pass and directly separates binding-to-content from binding-to-position, and it can *reveal* a feature that is silent only because of where it sat in the file. The failure mode to keep in mind is the one the authors leave open — once the mouth path is moved away, a red collar (in shape and proximity terms the next best candidate) inherits the mouth feature completely, so a feature that is "coherent" under one ordering can track proximity under another and the two readings are not separable without a position control. (transformer-circuits-pub-2025-october-update-index-html)

- **An overlap score over active units is length-dependent by construction, so publish the pooling rule and a position-matched null, not just a length-matched one**: the September 2025 re-audit is the folder's own worked case — the lab's cross-language feature-similarity finding came with an unexplained upward trend in intersection-over-union as the sample got longer, and they tested it properly against their own prior by requiring a last-sentence > first-sentence skew for related pairs *and* the reverse skew for unrelated pairs. Both signs landed as the model story predicts, but the contrast they used is confounded in the direction of their hypothesis, because a late-position pair has more conditioning text whether or not anything converges in meaning; the fix is to estimate the chance level at each position and subtract it rather than comparing related-at-A with unrelated-at-B. This applies directly to us, since feature-set Jaccard, top-k overlap and circuit node/edge overlap all drift with the number of firing tokens in exactly this way, and every "N% of the same features fire" claim in this folder inherits the problem. Two habits to import: specify the sign each control must take under the rival explanation before running it, which makes the control falsifiable rather than reassuring; and prefer the four-cell design (related/unrelated × position/format) so the two diagonal cells carry the theory and the two off-diagonal cells carry the instrument, at the cost of one extra pass over data you already have. (transformer-circuits-pub-2025-september-update-index-html)

- **An intervention on a latent direction belongs in the training graph, not at the readout — and the way to prove it worked is to publish the data-side skyline against yourself**: the ablation-fine-tuning work is the best template in this folder for turning a direction claim into a counterfactual prediction, because a projection spliced into the forward *and* backward pass means the model is trained never to route through the abolished concept, so the resulting behaviour is not a mask that can be lifted at inference. Two by-products are worth more than the result. First, the **checkpoint Pareto frontier**: any intervention that perturbs training makes the model worse, and the cheapest way to reduce a fine-tuning side effect is to fine-tune less, so the honest baseline is a shorter run, not the unmodified one. Second, they publish the ceiling they are measured against — 100 benign completions, 2% of the data, reproducing the same order of improvement — which is the discipline almost every steering paper in this folder skips; if a data patch matches a mechanism patch, the mechanism patch's value is explanatory rather than practical, and saying so is the difference between an honest paper and a sales pitch. (arxiv-2507.16795, arxiv-2510.20487, alignment-anthropic-com-2025-distill-paraphrases)
- **When the control that isolates your method's key step comes within noise of the method, that is the finding, not the baseline** — the interpretation step is where a direction-selection method is supposed to earn its keep, and the ablation-fine-tuning paper's own uninterpreted top-k ablation lands "surprisingly close" to the hand-read set on one of two models and behaves like early stopping on the other. This is the second time in this folder that a *selection* protocol was shown to be doing less work than advertised (the feature trees in the classifier paper were weaker but no less accurate; here the uninterpreted directions are nearly as effective), and the pattern generalises into a standing demand: for any ranking-plus-reading pipeline we build, run the rank-only arm and report the gap explicitly, because "we interpreted the top k" is a claim about human reading and it has to be priced. (arxiv-2507.16795, transformer-circuits-pub-2024-features-as-classifiers-index-html)
- **A handful of low-level lexical directions can carry a global behavioural shift — which supports directions as handles and says nothing about them as the mechanism** — five to fifteen principal components or latents per 32B-class model, read as crime, violence and disease, were enough to cut misaligned answers to unrelated personal questions by an order of magnitude in two models, and a projection onto them is cheap. But the same directions failed on the task that needed two grammatical concepts separated, and one of the two models' improvement came almost entirely from a single question about gender roles. The honest reading is the one this folder keeps arriving at from different angles: a direction can be a real, reusable control knob on a behaviour while remaining an incomplete account of how the behaviour is computed, so the strength of the claim has to be set per *question asked*, not per direction found. (arxiv-2507.16795, anthropic-com-research-evaluating-feature-steering, arxiv-2511.00617)

- **Self-report is not a degraded copy of the mechanism, it is a different object trained on a different signal** — the arithmetic case is the sharpest version we have: the model adds via a coarse-approximation path plus a separate last-digit path, and when asked how it added it narrates the school carrying algorithm it is not running. The chain-of-thought case generalises the point in both directions — a claimed calculation can leave *no* computation features at all on a hard item, and a hinted answer produces intermediate steps built backwards from the target — so a verbalised trace is best modelled as a simulation of written explanations rather than as a low-resolution read-out of computation. For us this rules out two habits at once: using a model's stated strategy as the ground truth a probe must recover, and treating CoT faithfulness as a noise level to be estimated rather than a separate causal channel to be traced and intervened on. (anthropic-com-research-tracing-thoughts-language-model, anthropic-com-research-reasoning-models-dont-say-think, anthropic-com-research-measuring-faithfulness-in-chain-of-thought-reasoning)
- **A graph can be right about what does *not* matter and still miss the cause — and that partial state should be reported, not hidden** — the failed-jailbreak post-mortem is the cleanest case: no making-bomb-specific feature exists, the attributions from the "make" token are essentially zero, and tracing backwards dies in error nodes, yet the near-zero attribution is itself a correct, falsifiable prediction (swap "make" for "detect" and the refusal is unchanged, sentence for sentence). This is a third outcome alongside "explained" and "no explanation found", and it is the one a reader is most likely to misread as success. For us it means any explanation artefact we ship should carry an explicit verdict per node: causally confirmed, merely correlated, or unaccounted (error/dark-matter), and that a report which names no upstream cause but predicts a null intervention is graded higher than one that names a confident wrong cause. The paired warning from the same note is that a graph's *silence* at a token is checkable in one edit, so cheap black-box substitution — not a larger graph — is the right next move. (transformer-circuits-pub-2025-april-update-index-html, anthropic-com-research-open-source-circuit-tracing)
- **A direction's firing context does not predict its intervention effect, and the mismatch is the thing to measure** — the bias-steering audit is the cleanest statement of this we have anywhere: every feature label in it was generated by auto-labelling the *contexts where the feature fires*, and the paper is an argument that this is a description of a topic rather than of a behaviour. Concretely, a "gender bias awareness" direction pushed gender-bias scores the label's opposite way and raised age bias on contexts it does not fire on, and on immigration questions a *pro-life* direction outperformed the immigration-specific direction roughly fivefold. This is the exact inversion of the habit our read-outs invite — pull the max-activating examples, name the concept, assume the name tells you what steering will do — so any direction we present as an explanation should carry two separate artefacts: its firing set (what it means) and a dose × evaluation matrix (what it does). Related and complementary: the thin direction found by one contrast pair catching sleeper agents is evidence that intervention relevance can exist where the concept story is unstated. (anthropic-com-research-evaluating-feature-steering, anthropic-com-research-probes-catch-sleeper-agents, goodfire-com-research-reward-hacking-activation-monitors)
- **Cross-category generality is a width the intervention does not have to predict away — the rare benign-broad direction** — against the off-target warnings elsewhere in this folder, the neutrality and multiple-perspectives directions cut bias across *all nine* measured social dimensions while holding task accuracy flat, i.e. one direction with a broad, desirable effect rather than one narrow handle and eight surprises. The interesting quantity is therefore not "is this direction entangled" but "how many independent evaluations does it move, and in which direction", which is the inverse of a specificity test and exactly what a cross-product grid makes measurable. (anthropic-com-research-evaluating-feature-steering, arxiv-2605.12412)
- **The interpretable readout wins or loses on the pooling rule, not on interpretability** — the feature-versus-raw-activation comparison flips sign between taking the maximum over the context and taking the last token, and the max-over-context version that wins on synthetic sets is also the one the authors suspect of exploiting spurious correlations, because it hands the classifier about two orders of magnitude more active values per prompt. For us this means a probe score is not a property of the probe alone: pooling is part of the hypothesis, and any claim that a white-box readout beats a black-box baseline must be run under both and must report the column where it loses.
- **A legible direction with a plausible story attached is the exact shape of a dataset artefact** — the harmless-voting feature read as "academic publication style implies innocuous academic exchange", which is a story anyone would accept, and it turned out to be a formatting correlation in synthetic training data that generalised across datasets without being about the task. The tell is not implausibility but *off-taskness*: nothing about the concept bears on harm. The cheap test that catches it is mechanical — firing rate per evaluation set split by true label, then zero-and-retrain, then append the triggering text to inputs and watch the prediction move.
- **Agreement between read-out methods at the top of the list is not agreement about the unit** — in the successor-head work three very different methods (weights, ICA, ablation) put the same heads first and then disagree below that, and the fast surrogate for ablation (attribution patching) reproduces only rank 1. The pattern has now recurred across several entries: the ranking is reliable for the handful of top units and unreliable in the tail, so a study that reports a single rank correlation over all units is quietly claiming its disagreements average out — they do not, and the tail is exactly where the low-layer high-ablation heads that no weight-side method found are living.
- **A metric that cannot separate two systems is reporting its own resolution, not a tie — and the honest output is a refusal to rank** — in the August 2024 monthly every non-vanilla SAE variant beat vanilla on the validated interpretability evals, and then all of them landed within noise of each other; rather than pick a winner on decimals, the authors say the evals lack the resolution for that comparison and defer to engineering properties (ease of use, robustness under scaling, ease of circuit read-out). The paired case is the sensitivity study a month earlier, where a dictionary's own showcase features sat in the top 0.1–10% but were mostly not the most sensitive. Both are the same lesson for us: when two of our read-outs differ by less than the instrument's spread, the correct claim is "indistinguishable by this measure", stated next to the measure's resolution, and the ranking has to come from a *different* kind of evidence such as a behavioural prediction. (transformer-circuits-pub-2024-august-update-index-html, transformer-circuits-pub-2024-july-update-index-html)
- **The adversarial version of "is this direction interpretable" is its cosine nearest neighbour, not a random control** — the sort eval's two sampling rules differ only in how the second feature is chosen, and the random version is nearly free to pass because unrelated directions have unrelated examples; picking the decoder-cosine neighbour instead makes the reader distinguish directions that a growing dictionary has *split*, which is the failure mode that matters once a dictionary is large enough to carve one concept into several seed-stable pieces. Any comparison across dictionary sizes we run should therefore be done on the hard pairing, or a bigger dictionary will appear better precisely because it over-splits. (transformer-circuits-pub-2024-august-update-index-html, transformer-circuits-pub-2024-june-update-index-html)
- **Letting the model narrate its own direction is cheap, layer-limited, and fails by naming the wrong sense** — decoder-vector injection at a prompting slot produces crisp labels for early, simple features and progressively worse ones as depth and model size grow, and on a larger model the failure is silent: the completion is fluent and true of a *different* sense of the polysemous concept (a spreadsheet "sheet" explained as a flat material, a code "(" as fastening) rather than obviously wrong. Because it needs no judge model it is a genuinely independent cross-check on autointerp, but its output has to be treated as a hypothesis about a feature, tested against the feature's own firing set, and swept over injection scale — scale sensitivity is itself a validity signal, since the feature that only names the right thing at extreme multipliers is not reporting a meaning. (transformer-circuits-pub-2024-august-update-index-html)
- **Reading instruments cost data movement, not compute — so cache everything the later analysis needs at collection time** — Anthropic's own account of scaling to *Scaling Monosemanticity* says the published science is the thin end of a pipeline dominated by shuffling, activation caching and re-partitioning so that scattered examples can be gathered: their first correct distributed shuffle read the whole 100 TB corpus once per worker, and their visualisation pipeline needed an extra dataset-sharded pass purely because a feature's most interesting tokens are spread across the corpus while a feature-sharded pass can only look in one place. The design rule that falls out is that any read-out instrument should record, in the same pass, every derived quantity a later claim will need (activation, gradient of the target logit, surrounding context window) rather than planning to revisit the corpus; a second scattered pass is the usual reason a white-box study stalls. (anthropic-com-research-engineering-challenges-interpretability, transformer-circuits-pub-2024-june-update-index-html)
- **A dictionary cannot be scored by itself — score what it lets you predict under intervention** — the June 2024 update states the field's own conclusion plainly: every SAE number (L0/MSE, loss recovered, density, even a blind human rubric) is a proxy, and an approach that removes shrinkage can improve MSE while leaving the underlying basis unchanged, so a dictionary has to be judged on whether it supports a causal claim. The reusable protocol comes from the outside IOI critique it digests: sufficiency (reconstruction preserves the task), necessity (the discarded reconstruction error cannot do the task), sparse controllability (a small edit set produces a predicted output change), and interpretability against named attributes. That four-part test is the shape our evaluation should take, because every part of it is a *prediction about behaviour*, not a statistic about the dictionary. (transformer-circuits-pub-2024-june-update-index-html, transformer-circuits-pub-2024-april-update-index-html)
- **"No feature for X" is a salience artefact before it is an absence** — the IOI work finds features for most indirect-object names but very few subject names across many hyperparameters, and attributes the asymmetry to the indirect objects having larger magnitude, calling it feature occlusion; the sibling failure is over-splitting, where one binary attribute comes apart into several opaque but seed-stable features. Both corrupt the two inferences we most want to make from a dictionary — "absent" and "one concept, one direction" — and both have the same signature: the split pieces are *reproducible across seeds* while the merged reading is not, and the occluded direction sits systematically lower in norm. Before reporting either, compare per-attribute effect magnitudes and check whether the fine-grained pieces replicate; a missing feature at small magnitude is not evidence of a missing concept. (transformer-circuits-pub-2024-june-update-index-html, transformer-circuits-pub-2024-march-update-index-html, anthropic-com-research-mapping-mind-language-model)
- **Attribution, not activation, is the cheap causal proxy — and the gap between them is large and measured** — across two independent Anthropic reports (Claude 3 Sonnet SAEs and the March 2024 feature-first circuit recipe) the same ordering holds: a graded attribution (gradient of a chosen logit difference · the direction · the activation) recovers ablation effects at r ≈ 0.81 while raw activation recovers them at r ≈ 0.12, and in a worked multi-step prompt only 3 of the 10 most-active features were among the 10 highest-ablation ones versus 8 of 10 by attribution. Any reading instrument we build should be ranked by attribution by default and treat "which features are burning" as a headline rather than a mechanism; activation is a popularity signal, not a causal one. (anthropic-com-research-mapping-mind-language-model, transformer-circuits-pub-2024-march-update-index-html, transformer-circuits-pub-2024-april-update-index-html)
- **Effects that only appear at 10× the observed range are a scale artefact until proven otherwise** — Sonnet's feature steering routinely required clamps outside the training distribution and collapsed into token repetition at ±100×, and the paper explains the need as co-active correlated features rather than as evidence that the direction is normally load-bearing. The check we should adopt before claiming a mechanism from an intervention is monotonicity: does the behavioural effect appear inside the range where the direction naturally fires, and does it turn over rather than explode just outside it? (anthropic-com-research-mapping-mind-language-model, goodfire-com-research-under-the-hood-of-a-reasoning-model)
- **A dictionary's nominal size is not its resolution — the live-feature count is** — the 34M Sonnet dictionary was 65% dead and is best treated as ~12M live directions, and once frequency curves are rescaled by live features they collapse onto one sigmoid whose midpoint sits just below the reciprocal of that count, giving an explicit prediction (a concept seen once per billion tokens needs ~a billion live features) and an explicit null result for "no feature for X". We should report discarded/dead units alongside every dictionary number, because per-feature statistics computed over empty slots are silently biased. (anthropic-com-research-mapping-mind-language-model, transformer-circuits-pub-2024-feb-update-index-html)

- **A recovered direction that is seed-stable across random inits is not therefore a feature — check it against constant offsets first** — the ultralow-density encoder vectors that looked like a mysterious shared concept were an artefact of the pre-encoder bias: because the input has a constant non-zero component, a unit can load opposite-signed weights and manufacture a negative bias, and units that never find a decoder direction drift into exactly that configuration in every seed. Seed stability is the test we would naturally use to certify a probe direction, so this is a false positive we would inherit; before calling a direction a feature, residualise it against the mean/constant component and ask which input units carry it. (transformer-circuits-pub-2024-feb-update-index-html, transformer-circuits-pub-2023-may-update-index-html)
- **Faithfulness and legibility can move in opposite directions, and one number will not show it** — a saturating penalty lifted the loss-recovered frontier outright while pushing the highest-firing features past 10% density and making the dictionary much harder to read; the field's own recipe book therefore contains a documented case where the metric everyone optimises improved and the thing everyone wants got worse. Report the pair (reconstruction/loss recovered *and* a legibility or density measure) or the optimisation will quietly pick the wrong one. (transformer-circuits-pub-2024-feb-update-index-html, transformer-circuits-pub-2024-jan-update-index-html)
- **A bug that improves the loss is a fact about the method, not a reason to keep the bug** — ghost grads were applied to every unit at training start by mistake and that beat the correct version, and the authors respond by downgrading the technique's scope (big win at one layer, little at larger scale) rather than defending it. The transferable habit is to treat any unexplained gain as a missing control: if we cannot say why an intervention helps, we do not know on which models it will stop helping. (transformer-circuits-pub-2024-feb-update-index-html, transformer-circuits-pub-2024-jan-update-index-html)
- **Making a representation "cleaner" can make it unreadable — check the direction of the trade before optimising** — forcing sparse activations on a model removes superposition and produces, by turns, cross-layer superposition (a unit whose meaning is only recoverable by reading it together with a residual stream that has no privileged basis) and magnitude-as-symbol (one axis carrying several discrete features at different magnitudes, decoded through the softmax). Anthropic's own summary of the lesson is a proverb they borrow: out of the frying pan and into the fire. The pattern to apply to our probes is that any interpretability intervention — sparsifying a readout, projecting onto a dictionary, picking a basis — needs both failure modes tested explicitly, because a cleaner-looking activation is not evidence that we have moved toward the model's representation rather than toward its data's. (transformer-circuits-pub-2024-jan-update-index-html)
- **"Interpretable" is a floor, not a result — and the field's own failure cases are the cheapest calibration for it** — on MNIST classifiers, dictionary learning returned digit features that looked right, had correct class logits downstream, and were still not demonstrably the model's features: no elbow in reconstruction error, no evidence of completeness, and good reconstruction needing a dictionary the size of the dataset. The same authors then scale the method to billions of parameters without a trusted quality metric, relying on human rubric and model-predicted tokens. The transferable habit is to report interpretability *together with* an explicit completeness claim or none at all, and to treat "the features make sense" as one step above "we found nothing". (anthropic-com-research-decomposing-language-models-into-understandable-components, transformer-circuits-pub-2024-jan-update-index-html)
- **The test that distinguishes a model property from a data property is cheap and we should run it every time** — shuffle the trained weights (keeping the weight distributions identical) and re-run the analysis; if the structure survives, it was in the corpus. Both a dictionary decomposition and a feature-level readout can pass the "looks meaningful" bar while being a re-description of dataset correlations projected into a new space, and the authors' own defence — that downstream effects were never an input to the fitting procedure, so agreement between them and the activations must come from the model — is a clean argument that our probes should reuse: fit on one thing, validate on a quantity the fit never saw. Cross-model replication of the same direction (median 0.72 across seeds against 0.46 for neurons) is the second half of that test. (anthropic-com-research-decomposing-language-models-into-understandable-components, transformer-circuits-pub-2024-jan-update-index-html)
- **A behaviour the model can only reach one way in the input is not the same as a relation it has learned** — the order-swap test separates the two with one edit, and the decisive variant is not the reversal itself but *deleting the contextual half of the reversed sequence*: if the influence survives, what the model has is the string, not the association. Any of our probes or attributions that claims a compositional or relational feature should be reported alongside the swapped and half-deleted versions, because a predictive white-box model that gets the correct-order case right and the swapped case wrong has learned a co-occurrence and nothing more. (anthropic-com-research-studying-large-language-model-generalization-with-influence-functions)
- **Attribution is heavy-tailed and diffuse at the same time, and both numbers are needed** — a power-law tail with an exponent low enough to make even the variance infinite, sitting next to "no single example moves the output by one nat" and "the top 1% carries 12–52% of the mass", is the signature of a behaviour supported by very many weak contributors. Report the pair, never the tail shape alone: "sparse influence" and "memorised from a handful of sequences" are different claims and the second is falsified by the second number. It is also the right way to describe how concentrated our own probe evidence is. (anthropic-com-research-studying-large-language-model-generalization-with-influence-functions, goodfire-com-research-probe-based-data-attribution)
- **Depth specialises even when it averages flat** — influence over the MLP layers is near-uniform on average across 25,000 scores, yet recall queries live in the upper layers and abstraction, code, maths and translation in the middle, so any pool-everything readout blurs precisely the structure that carries the meaning. The same caution applies to any mean-over-layers probe we build, and it independently corroborates the repeated finding that mid-stack is where abstract, transferable content sits. (anthropic-com-research-studying-large-language-model-generalization-with-influence-functions, anthropic-com-research-scaling-laws-and-interpretability-of-learning-from-repeated-data)

- **Text-level faithfulness has no ground truth, so the two natural metric families must be run in opposite directions**: truncation sensitivity (does the answer need the trace?) and corruption sensitivity (does damaging the trace change the answer?) together separate a trace generated *after* the answer from a trace that is read but not decisive — and neither can distinguish a model that reasoned honestly from one whose internal state was already committed before it wrote anything. A probe reading the same hidden cause without text is the missing third opinion, and is the right validation partner for any "the model used its reasoning" claim. (anthropic-com-research-question-decomposition-improves-the-faithfulness-of-model-generated-reasoning, anthropic-com-research-measuring-faithfulness-in-chain-of-thought-reasoning)
- **Name the influence class before claiming a faithfulness intervention works**: an explicit user suggestion and an all-"A" few-shot answer distribution are both "biasing contexts" from the same cited paper, yet decomposition fixes only the first (21.3 → 9.2 points) and moves nothing on the second, and disclosure rates on unauthorized-information hints differ by model in the opposite direction to the overall rate. Faithfulness is a vector over influence classes, not a scalar; a paper that improves a mean over mixed classes has shown almost nothing. (anthropic-com-research-question-decomposition-improves-the-faithfulness-of-model-generated-reasoning, anthropic-com-research-measuring-faithfulness-in-chain-of-thought-reasoning)
- **Silence and longer-is-honest**: unfaithful traces are *longer* than faithful ones and the unfaithful case is typically a fluent, well-formed rationale for a wrong answer rather than a terse omission — so trace length, fluency and self-consistency are anti-signals for honesty, and any monitor credited with detecting deception by scoring trace quality is measuring the wrong thing. (anthropic-com-research-measuring-faithfulness-in-chain-of-thought-reasoning)

- **Effective dimensionality of a reference activation set** (D_i = ‖h_i‖²/Σ_j(ĉ·h_j)², plus a maximal variant that takes a supremum over probe directions) turns "is this representation organised around features or around particular examples?" into a label-free number computable from activations alone, and a heavy tail over inputs is a candidate special-casing / memorisation detector. Requires no known feature list, so it is available on a black-box model. (transformer-circuits-pub-2023-toy-double-descent-index-html)
- **Same weights, two computational strategies, one of them invisible in the obvious basis**: a feature-vector scatter that looks like unstructured noise can be a clean example-polytope, so "no feature structure found" is not "no mechanism". Any weight-side reading needs at least two coordinate systems (feature basis vs data-point basis) before it can report a negative. (transformer-circuits-pub-2023-toy-double-descent-index-html, transformer-circuits-pub-2023-may-update-index-html)
- **Occam against reconstruction error as the rule for how many components an account may have**: plot reconstruction/distortion against the description length of the decomposition (entropy of the code and dictionary entries) and take the corner of that frontier; validate the rule on synthetic data with a known factor count and a ground-truth recovery score before applying it to a model whose answer is unknown. Turns "how large should our basis be" from a hyperparameter into a testable claim. (transformer-circuits-pub-2023-may-update-index-html, transformer-circuits-pub-2022-solu-index-html)
- **A detected direction can be a fraction of a mechanism**: when mechanisms are stored in superposition across units (heads here, neurons in the earlier posts), each unit alone implements a corrupted mixture, so per-unit ablations can understate or misattribute the mechanism while a marginal reading looks valid. Circuit claims need an interference/coverage control, not just a successful single-unit ablation. (transformer-circuits-pub-2023-may-update-index-html, arxiv-2607.12279)
- **Regime boundaries as the predictive object**: memorisation-versus-generalisation, and clean-versus-messy superposition geometry, are both predicted by simple data statistics (recurrence rate ~10 per feature; repeated-point threshold ~T/N; feature pressure; training length). A white-box model that predicts *which strategy* a network will adopt is a stronger claim than one that fits losses inside a single regime. (transformer-circuits-pub-2023-toy-double-descent-index-html, transformer-circuits-pub-2023-may-update-index-html)
- **Offset, not zero-centre**: real activations plausibly satisfy A = SD + b, and the offset is a free coordinate that changes the geometry of a decomposition (and of memorisation; the toy posts normalise examples to unit norm, which quiets this). Report it, and check whether a finding survives re-centring. (transformer-circuits-pub-2023-may-update-index-html)
- **Mean-difference direction between a behaviour and a broad contrast set**, validated by checking it is the leading PCA component, is the standard construction for behavioural axes (Assistant Axis; refusal direction; weight steering). (anthropic-com-research-assistant-axis, arxiv-2511.05408)
- Axis position as a *predictor of future behaviour* (compliance after persona drift; emergence of misalignment) — the closest thing in the field to prediction under intervention from a single number. (anthropic-com-research-assistant-axis)
- Ceiling/capping at the empirically normal activation range, rather than constant steering, preserves capability while changing behaviour. (anthropic-com-research-assistant-axis)
- **Base-model control readout** is the standard null for any elicitation claim: same readout from the untuned model must show no benefit. (arxiv-2510.01070)
- Auditor/guess-the-secret grading, with an I/O-only baseline, is now the default evaluation shape for hidden-knowledge work. (arxiv-2510.01070, arxiv-2603.05494)
- Tokens (logit lens / embedding-nearest) beat SAE prose descriptions when the auditor needs a discrete answer. (arxiv-2510.01070)
- Caveat to remember: readouts can be empty because the info was never computed on that pass (refusals short-circuit). (arxiv-2510.01070)
- Repeated claim: the model carries a **representation of the truth independent of what it outputs** (probes trained on unrelated honesty data transfer to censorship). Ground truth for this = "knows X, says not-X". (arxiv-2603.05494)
- Abliteration / mean-difference refusal-or-behaviour direction removal at 50-75% depth is the standard cheap white-box intervention baseline across safety papers. (arxiv-2603.05494, arxiv-2511.05408)
- **Text-inversion control**: any benchmark for "model explains itself from internals" must ensure the answer is not derivable from the visible context. (arxiv-2606.02609)
- Prose explanations get judged on separate hallucination vs vagueness axes with chance-adjusted scores; end-to-end readers are still weaker than a linear probe on narrow tasks. (arxiv-2606.02609)
- Activations are most informative around 60-70% depth and on-policy data is the preferred training/test distribution. (arxiv-2606.02609)
- Loss frontiers (retain vs forget, behaviour vs capability) rather than single-point accuracy as the headline result shape; both sides evaluated at matched values of the other. (alignment-anthropic-2025-selective-gradient-masking, arxiv-2511.05408)
- Removal/ablation must be tested by *recovery cost* (how much retraining restores the capability), because loss can hide suppression rather than removal. (alignment-anthropic-2025-selective-gradient-masking)
- Ground-truth toy models as the testing ground for decomposition methods: both SPD and attributions-of-decomposition papers build toy models with known mechanisms and score recovery (MMCS/L2 ratio). (arxiv-2506.20790)
- Shrinkage is the recurring failure mode of learned decompositions, in activations and in weights; need an explicit magnitude metric alongside cosine similarity. (arxiv-2506.20790)
- "Contrastive subtraction" is the field's default way to isolate a behaviour: D+ vs D- fine-tunes (weights), persona pairs (activations). Shared nuisance factors cancel. (arxiv-2511.05408)
- Behaviour metric x capability metric, both evaluated OOD, with a magnitude dial (k) and OOD-generalisation as the headline comparison. (arxiv-2511.05408)
- Weight-space directions are emerging as monitors (cosine similarity of a fine-tune update to a reference direction), separate from and cheaper than activation probes. (arxiv-2511.05408)
- **Difference-in-means axis built from synthetic feedback data, validated four ways** (held-out AUROC, cross-layer cosine, logit lens, causal steering) is the current gold-standard recipe for claiming a direction means something. (arxiv-2606.17056, anthropic-com-research-assistant-axis)
- A single linear macro-variable ("am I on track", "am I drifting from persona") projects on the last prompt token and works as an input-free monitor; post-training silently re-writes it, so monitors drift. (arxiv-2606.17056, anthropic-com-research-assistant-axis)
- Logit lens / unembedded-token readout remains a strong cheap baseline, and its accuracy is layer-dependent with a peak in mid-to-late layers (a "semantic hub"); always sweep layers. (arxiv-2512.01222, arxiv-2606.17056)
- Model organisms are built by *re-encoding existing outputs* and fine-tuning — fast, but it makes the test easier than the real phenomenon; state the confound. (arxiv-2512.01222, arxiv-2603.05494)
- **Swap intervention** (replace direction A with equal-norm direction B) is a stronger causal test than ablation: it shows the downstream computation *reads from* the location, not merely that removing it hurts. (anthropic-com-research-global-workspace)
- A small broadcast channel (few dozen concepts, <10% of activity) plus a large automatic periphery as the two-mode structure of network computation; routine behaviour survives deletion, composition/reasoning does not. (anthropic-com-research-global-workspace)
- Jacobian-of-future-logits lens (J-lens) generalises logit lens from "next token" to "any word it might say later" and finds reportable contents. (anthropic-com-research-global-workspace, arxiv-2512.01222)
- Eval-awareness is a confound in *every* safety eval: ablating "fake/fictional" style patterns changed measured behaviour, i.e. good behaviour was partly conditional on knowing it was tested. (anthropic-com-research-global-workspace, arxiv-2606.17056)
- Heads are the wrong unit: heads are polysemantic *without* superposition because their output is high-rank, and some behaviours are high-rank; the target unit is a sparse, high-rank "attentional feature". (transformer-circuits-pub-2026-headvis-index-html)
